***

**Notes on transcription:**
*   **ſ**: long s.
*   **ę**: e-caudata, representing *ae*.
*   **⁹**: abbreviation for *-us* (e.g., *ſumend⁹* for *sumendus*).
*   **q₃**: abbreviation for *-que*.
*   **ꝑ**: abbreviation for *per* or *pro* (in *ꝑpter* for *propter*).
*   **ꞇ**: (in *coquunꞇ*) represents a 't' with a curl, a common abbreviation for the ending *-ur*.
*   **~** (macron/tilde): indicates a missing 'm' or 'n' (e.g., *ſūmend⁹* for *sumendus*, *cōditus* for *conditus*).
*   **[red pilcrow]**: A red paragraph mark at the beginning of the header.
*   **c.i.**: Signature mark at the bottom right of the page.

Wait, line 16: `imaginū, i`. The 'i' at the end of the line is the start of `in` or `im-`.
Line 17: `Dęmonū`.
So it's `imaginū, i / Dęmonū`. This doesn't make sense.

Line 17: `Dęmonū`.
Maybe it's `imaginū, i / Dęmonū` = `imaginum in Daemonum`?
Let's re-read: "Referre autem mirabiles quosdam effectus imaginum in Daemonum falsitatem". Yes, that makes sense.
